CRM Brief
Document CRM Brief — catalogue #58
Engagement Jewell Projects × Tap That Brewery
Phase 02 Design
Status Draft v01 — Internal
Date 19 August 2026
Prepared by Jewell Projects

Pre-CP1 draft — produced ahead of Discover sign-off; census data, membership pricing reconciliation and competitor verification may revise inputs.

The job

Complete the GoTab (POS) → Fishbowl (CRM) integration and stand up the lifecycle engine on top of it. This is the single highest-leverage build in the programme: every month it slips, retention stays a hand-sent monthly SMS and the warm list of 220 actives leaks refill revenue. It gates #57 Sequence 2 and the #56 scale-up (see critical path, #61).

Integration scope

In scope Detail
Transaction sync Every GoTab keg transaction (20L/5L/50L refill, corny fill, system, membership, Benchy) to the Fishbowl customer record, daily or better
Identity resolution One customer = one record across POS, web forms (#53), giveaway entries, Tap Tokens; dedupe rule on mobile then email
Computed lifecycle state days_since_last_keg → Active <75 / At-risk 75–90 / Churned 90+, recalculated nightly
Trigger events out Day-60 / 75 / 90 events fire #57 automations (email + SMS), replacing the manual monthly SMS
Web capture in demo_booked, giveaway_entry, function_enquiry land as records with consent timestamp + UTM source
Out of scope (phase 2) Uber Direct delivery data, lease-to-buy contract handling, franchise reporting

The 60/75/90 trigger set (replaces manual SMS)

Trigger Fires Condition guards
Day 60 Nudge email (#57) Skip if a purchase or an open winback offer exists
Day 75 At-risk SMS + email Tag cohort for save-rate reporting (#19 KR 2.2)
Day 90 Winback SMS (offer ladder) Offer variant recorded per send; suppress after 2 winback cycles without response [cadence TBC]

Required fields

customer_id · name · mobile · email · suburb/postcode · consent status/date/source · segment self-select (backyard/shed/office/club) · own_system_elsewhere (Y/N) — the switcher flag, set at first fill (#19 KR 1.1) · system_owned (type, purchase date, bought-from-us Y/N) · membership tier + expiry · last_keg_date · lifecycle_state · lifetime keg count/value · Tap Token balance (or link) · referral source · winback offers sent/redeemed.

Required views (Raef + founders, no analyst)

  1. Active count this week — the one number (#24 P6): actives, trend, vs the 220 → 1,000 path.
  2. At-risk board — everyone at 60–90 days, last contact, next trigger due.
  3. Switcher intake — new actives flagged own_system_elsewhere, monthly (target ~7–8/month).
  4. Membership attach — memberships ÷ systems sold, monthly (target 60%).
  5. Giveaway cohort — entrants by cycle: nurture stage, demo booked, purchased.

Wholesale pipeline stages

Fixes the admitted DM-lead leakage. Simple pipeline, 48-hour first-follow-up SLA (#19 KR 3.4):

Lead (DM/referral) → Contact identified → Tasting/style-match sent → Trial keg → Pouring (account) → Dormant

Fields: venue name, style match (Japanese lager → Japanese restaurant etc.), decision-maker, source, next action + date. White-label club deals tracked in the same pipeline with a deal-type flag.

Test plan

  1. Sync integrity: 20 known transactions posted in GoTab appear correctly attached in Fishbowl within the sync window; target 100% attach rate on new transactions (#19 KR 2.4).
  2. Lifecycle maths: seed test customers at day 59/61/74/76/89/91; verify state transitions and that exactly the right triggers fire — and suppress on purchase.
  3. End-to-end: website demo form → record → consent stored → test sequence send → unsubscribe honoured → state visible in views.
  4. Backfill audit: historic customer base imported, last_keg_date computed, current 220-active figure reconciled against the client's own count before go-live.
  5. Manual cutover: run manual SMS and automation in parallel for one cycle; then manual stops.

Success criteria

Integration live; triggers replacing manual SMS after one parallel cycle; the five views populated and used in the weekly review; wholesale pipeline holding every open lead with an SLA date.

Assumptions & open items

  • GoTab/Fishbowl integration capability and timeline unconfirmed — the named production gate from D06. If it slips past Stage 1, triggers run semi-manually off exported lists (per #24).
  • Data ownership/API access, historic data quality, and Tap Tokens system-of-record [TBC].
  • Winback suppression cadence and day-75 offer economics [TBC with client].

Feeds into: #57 EDM Brief, #56 Paid Media Brief (scale gate + first_fill feedback), #60 Events Brief (capture flows), #61 Final Brief Pack.

The job

Earned media that does what paid can't: credibility during the 3-month buying cycle and backlinks for #55. Three stories, pitched in this order. The Gold Coast Bulletin has already covered the founding ("Shaken but not stirred") — there is a proven local appetite; build on it, don't repeat it.

Story 1 — The comeback + the award (lead)

Angle: Head brewer Chris Smith was at Burleigh Barrels when it collapsed owing staff. He rebuilt across town — and Tap That Brewery's Midnight in Tokyo (black Japanese lager) took Brewer's Choice at Crafted in their first year entering. Redemption arc + hometown pride + a product you can taste this weekend. Hooks: award anniversary; next Crafted entry; new beer releases off the "Crafting Soon" board. Guardrails: Burleigh Barrels handled factually and generously — Chris's story, not a takedown; "Australia's biggest beer festival" is the client's description of Crafted — verify before any release uses it.

Story 2 — The Benchy novelty (colour/lifestyle)

Angle: "Beer on tap in the middle of nowhere" — a Gold Coast brewery is putting draught beer into 4WDs, campsites, boats and caravans with a portable tap no one else in the country offers. Strong visual story (hero imagery from #54); first partner (a local 4WD fit-out business) gives it a second local face. Hooks: partner launch; summer camping season; caravan/camping show calendar [dates TBC]. Guardrails: responsible-consumption framing is mandatory — never drink-driving-adjacent; lean on the social-responsibility flyer commitments (supports programs for victims of drink driving) proactively.

Story 3 — The club white-label (community/sport)

Angle: Local sports clubs get their own beer — "Surfers Paradise Demons Lager" model: white-labelled club beer, 10% player discount, 10% back to the club. A sponsorship story where the money stays in the community, against the backdrop of big-brewer tap exclusivity. Hooks: first signed club (#19 KR 3.2); season launch; grand-final season. Guardrails: pitch only once a club is signed and pouring — no vapourware; do not name CUB or attack exclusivity deals directly, let the contrast speak.

The job

Events already work commercially (one recent ticketed night did a week's trade in five hours) — but under #24 P3 the taproom's job is conversion, not revenue. Every event on this slate must run the capture mechanics below; an event that fills the room without capturing a single system lead is a party, not marketing. Owner: Raef, inside his taproom quota.

Next-quarter slate (Sept–Nov 2026)

Event Date Type Primary marketing job
Father's Day × Smoked Barbecue Co (keg system + smoking masterclass voucher) Sun 6 Sept Retail collab System sales + outdoor-living JV proof point
Brazilian night (booked) Early Sept [date TBC] Ticketed music — organiser takes door, venue takes bar Full-room demo-corner trial + capture pilot
Reggae night / "duffs" [TBC] Oct Ticketed music Repeat the proven format; target the 250-standing goal
Giveaway cycle launch night [TBC — align #52 Kit 2 / #57 Seq 1] Owned promo Entry volume + switcher flagging
After-work local business session [TBC] monthly Owned — happy hour "Knockoffs" extension Industrial-area workplaces segment; function/office-system leads
Taps & Tales brewery tour (members) + Hop On tour slots Ongoing Partner/member Membership value + review generation

Private functions (minimum-spend model, 1 drink/person/hour benchmark) continue as revenue but sit outside this brief except for capture mechanics.

Per-event run sheet requirements

Every event gets a one-page run sheet, filed in advance:

  1. Purpose in one line (which funnel it feeds) + target numbers (attendance, entries, demos, member signups).
  2. Roster + who owns the demo corner (venue-manager cover rule applies — Raef is not behind the bar during peak capture windows).
  3. Capacity plan (mezzanine ~40 / downstairs ~70 / ~120–130 seated / standing target) + RSA compliance notes.
  4. Food answer (Ammazza/snacks or truck confirmed — the known friction point; never left to the night).
  5. Promo checklist: event listed on GBP + socials + #57 newsletter; photo capture list for #54/#55 (with signage/consent notice at entry).
  6. Post-event: numbers logged within 48 hours (see measurement).

System-sales capture mechanics (the point of the brief)

Mechanic How it runs
Demo corner A working 2-tap system ($975, at-cost story, Afterpay/Zip card) staffed at peak; staff line: the venue converts 20–30% of visitors who see it — the demo corner exists to force the "that's a keg system?" moment at events too. Demo bookings taken on the spot (QR → #53 form)
Giveaway entries QR entry at every table + door; entry form captures own-a-system Y/N (switcher flag → #58); entry pitch scripted into MC/host moments at ticketed nights
Member signups Keg Crew pitched at POS and demo corner once pricing is reconciled (#52 Kit 4) — until then, Brew Buds (free mailing-list tier) is the default ask so no event captures nothing
Switcher intercept One question in every demo-corner chat: "got a system at home already?" → We Fill Any System card + first-fill offer [TBC]

Measurement (per event, logged to #58 views)

  • Attendance + bar take (context, not the goal).
  • Giveaway/list entries; demo-corner conversations; demos booked; member signups; switcher flags collected.
  • 90-day trailer: systems sold and first fills traceable to the event cohort (via entry records).
  • Kill test: any recurring format producing zero captures across two runs is dropped or rebuilt, regardless of bar take.

Success criteria

  1. Every slated event has a filed run sheet and produces a logged capture count — no exceptions.
  2. Demo corner live at all owned + ticketed events from the Brazilian night onward.
  3. Quarter target [TBC at CP1]: proposed floor — 300 new consented list entries, 30 demos booked, 20 switcher flags across the slate.

Assumptions & open items

  • Dates beyond the booked Brazilian night and Father's Day are proposals; confirm with client calendar.
  • Food-truck roster / catering partner remains unsolved — flagged to client as a function-revenue gate, not solved here.
  • Capture targets and first-fill offer economics [TBC]; standing-event 250 target subject to licensing/capacity confirmation.

What this pack is

Nine execution briefs turn the Design-phase strategy (220 → 1,000 active keg customers; the switcher lane, the lifecycle engine, the funnel the venue already proves) into makeable work. This page is the index, the order of operations, and the sign-off. Owners marked [TBC] are assigned at CP1 — the capacity reality (Raef at ~70% marketing) means most builds are Jewell-led or contracted, with Raef owning ongoing operation.

The critical path

  1. Collateral fix first — this week. Two conflicting membership posters and a two-priced tasting paddle sit at the till of a brand charging $120–170 against $85–90 rivals on a quality story. Client confirms the live prices; #52 Kit 4 ships; every other brief inherits the reconciled list. Nothing customer-facing publishes before it.
  2. CRM + EDM before paid scale-up. The warm assets — 220 actives, ~1,000 giveaway entrants/cycle, the churned list that's "lapsed, not lost" — leak at 75–90 days while retention is one hand-sent SMS a month. #58 and #57 (Sequences 1–2) must be live before #56 spends beyond test budgets: paid fills the funnel; CRM/EDM stop it draining.
  3. Website + shoot make paid worth clicking. #54 and #53 land in wave 2 so wave-3 paid traffic hits real proof, not the current site.
  4. PR and Benchy ride the hooks. #59 and #52 Kit 3 fire when their assets and signings are real — hook-driven, not calendar-driven.

What we need from Tap That Brewery to start

Confirm live membership pricing + paddle price #52 Kit 4 → everything
GoTab/Fishbowl integration access + timeline #58 → #57 Seq 2 → #56 scale
Full census data + business docs (carried from discovery) Targets locking; copy claims
Budget envelopes (paid media, production, incentives) #56, #54, #57 offers
Customer intros for the shoot; Benchy pricing; giveaway date #54, #52 Kits 2–3

Summarises #43 Activation Plan, #45 Campaign Calendar, #46–51 channel plans, #33 Messaging & Offer Architecture, #61 Final Brief Pack

Pre-CP1 draft — produced ahead of Discover sign-off; census data, membership pricing reconciliation and competitor verification may revise inputs.

One system, one number

Every channel in this programme exists to move a single number: active keg customers (a keg bought within 75 days) — 220 today, toward 300 at six months and 450 at twelve, on the path to 1,000 (~$2.6M). Channels are not judged on their own metrics; they are judged on what they feed the refill engine. The structure is one always-on layer, four campaign pulses on top of it, and a lifecycle automation spine underneath everything.

The always-on layer (never stops)

Stream What runs Owner
Organic social 3 posts + 3–4 stories/week across the five pillars; UGC engine; community management Raef
Lifecycle messaging Manual monthly winback SMS until GoTab × Fishbowl lands; then automated 60/75/90-day triggers, onboarding, cadence reminders, renewals Jewell builds; Raef sends until automated
Local SEO Google Business Profile, "keg refills Gold Coast" / "we fill any system" pages, calculator, FAQ schema Jewell
Referral (Tap Tokens) 1,000 tokens/system · 1,000/venue · 500/refill — counter script, keg-box card, SMS footer, monthly leaderboard content Raef, with #44 collateral

The four campaign windows (Sep 2026 – Aug 2027)

Window Campaign Objective Close mechanic
Sep–Oct 2026 C1 — "We Fill Any System" (switcher) +20–30 net new actives with zero hardware giveaway — the cheapest CAC in the model (50% of the base already switched unprompted) First fill at member rate + switch bonus
Oct–Dec 2026 C2 — Giveaway cycle 1, with built nurture Beat the 22–25 systems/cycle baseline by giving ~975 non-winners a designed 90-day nurture instead of silence Proven 50%-off window, staged against Christmas install
Jan–Feb 2027 C3 — Giveaway cycle 2 (summer) Repeat the improved playbook; second data point on the nurture lift; the repeatability test 50%-off close before footy season
Mar–May 2027 C4 — Benchy outdoor launch 15–20 portable units into the zero-competitor 4WD/camping category, each a new refill account Bundle: unit + first fill; partner-channel led
Jun–Aug 2027 Winter consolidation — deliberately no headline campaign EOFY workplace push (light), club pipeline, renewals, automation tuning, cycle-3 prep —

The lifecycle automation spine (under every campaign)

Trigger Touch Replaces
System purchase Onboarding: setup → first-refill reminder → Keg Crew pitch → day-30 referral ask Nothing (new)
~21–25 days since last keg "What's Kegged & Ready" cadence email Nothing (new)
Day 60 Range/freshness nudge — no discount Nothing (new)
Day 75 (at-risk) SMS + email, service angle — still no discount The untargeted monthly blast
Day 90 (churned) Winback ladder: $20-off window → Marie's Pizza/Buttercard → next tested offer Today's hand-sent SMS
Giveaway entry 90-day nurture matched to the 3-month buying cycle Silence between entry and close

Channel roles and the interdependencies that govern sequence

Channel Role The rule (from #61 critical path)
Collateral (#52 Kit 4) Foundation First, this week. Conflicting membership posters and paddle prices reconcile before anything customer-facing publishes
CRM (#48) The machine — days-since-last-keg, switcher flag, provenance Wave 1, start now; interim manual export layer does not wait for the integration
EDM (#47) Highest-ROI channel — works lists the business already owns at ~$0 media Sequences 1–3 start manually; 4–6 ride the CRM build
Paid media (#46) Amplifier of proven mechanics only (Meta + Google Search; nothing else) Test budgets only until CRM + EDM are live — paid fills the funnel; CRM/EDM stop it draining
Website/SEO (#53/#55) Proof destination Live before paid scales, so traffic hits real proof
PR (#49) Earned-only credibility (comeback story, award, Benchy) One pitch/month max; no retainer while the P&L is red
Events (#50) The taproom earning its keep — every event scored as a funnel stage (contacts captured, systems attributed) Kill any format that captures nothing after three runs
Traditional (#51) Held. Signage/livery (infrastructure) excepted No paid traditional until paid digital survives two full giveaway-cycle kill criteria

One message architecture, every channel

Roof line: "Good times on tap at your place." The working rule from discovery applies everywhere: kudos and connection open; cost closes — cost is the customer's dirty little secret, framed per litre, private-voiced, never a sticker fight against $85–90 rivals. High-volume drinkers are the single exception where the 44-schooners maths leads.

Channel Opens with (Pillar 1–2: connection/kudos, freshness/award) Closes with (Pillar 3: cost/convenience)
Organic social & UGC Backyard hosting, install envy, her taps too, brewery floor (Rarely closes — feeds retargeting)
Meta cold "Your place becomes the default"; the mates' reaction —
Meta retargeting / close windows — 50%-off window, Afterpay/Zip, per-litre maths, census moderation stat
EDM nurture Objection ladder: her taps, footprint, moderation Days 61–90: the close + Keg Crew attach
PR Comeback story, first-year award, Benchy novelty (Never — PR is credibility, not offer)
Events The demo moment: "that's a keg system?" 5L mini $225 impulse exit; Afterpay signage

Governance rhythm

Cadence What happens Who
Weekly Active count (75-day rule), new actives by source, at-risk count — from GoTab Jewell pulls, all see
Monthly One-page report: actives vs 300/450 staging, campaign metrics, nurture conversion, referral redemptions, taproom→system conversion Jewell
Per campaign Kill/scale criteria per #46/#43 tables — decided on the numbers, not extended on hope Jewell + founders
Quarterly Event scorecard ranking (contacts + systems per staff-hour); automation trigger tuning Jewell + Raef

Vanity metrics — followers, entry volume without conversion, bar take as a lead metric — are explicitly excluded.

Summarises The full Design set #23–61, built on the Discover base (D06, #22)

Pre-CP1 draft — produced ahead of Discover sign-off; census data, membership pricing reconciliation and competitor verification may revise inputs.

What this document is

The Discover phase ended with a diagnosis: Tap That Brewery is a refill subscription business being run with a hospitality venue's attention — 220 active keg customers against a 1,000 target (~$2.6M), a loss-making P&L, and one marketer at ~70% capacity as the binding constraint. The Design phase turned that diagnosis into strategy, brand, channel plans and nine execution briefs. This page is what the founders are approving. Sign-off here is CP2: it closes Design and unlocks Deploy.

The strategic choices made — and what was deliberately rejected

# Chosen Rejected (on purpose, not by omission)
1 Compete in the home-tap category — "beer on tap at home", which nobody on the Gold Coast owns Becoming another Burleigh craft taproom brand — the most crowded fight in the region
2 Switchers first: win refills from the installed base of keg systems, whoever sold the hardware (50% of the current book already switched unprompted) Treating every new active as someone who must first be sold a $975 system
3 Kudos/connection open, cost closes — cost framed per litre, private-voiced, in retargeting and the close only (high-volume drinkers the sole exception) Price-led creative; any sticker fight against $85–90 rivals ($120–170 kegs lose it, and forfeit the award story doing so)
4 Lifecycle automation before new-audience spend — the 60/75/90-day engine on the lists the business already owns Cold-reach spend while the warm list leaks at 75–90 days
5 Taproom scoped as funnel, not profit centre — events scored on contacts captured and systems attributed Another 18 months of venue-first attention; bar take as a lead metric
6 Paid media = Meta + Google Search only, test-protocol posture, gated behind CRM/EDM TikTok, YouTube, display, Spotify; agency-normal always-on spend
7 Traditional media held (signage/livery infrastructure excepted) until paid digital survives two giveaway cycles TV, billboards, cinema, awareness print, always-on radio
8 Wholesale as white-label opportunism (10% player / 10% club, non-CUB, short named list) with a 48-hour follow-up SLA Broad wholesale prospecting; chasing CUB-locked venues
9 PR earned-only, one pitch/month, riding real hooks (comeback story, award, Benchy) PR agency retainer while the P&L is red
10 Brand voice named as Regular Guy × Jester — the voice already on the walls; puns earn the follow, straight talk earns the trust Inventing a new brand; letting the Jester price-joke the $975 trust decision

The priorities, in order (#24)

P1 Refill acquisition, switchers first · P2 Lifecycle automation before new-audience spend · P3 Re-weight effort from taproom to engine · P4 Wholesale as white-label opportunism only · P5 Collateral and pricing hygiene · P6 Measure the engine, not the noise.

The 12-month plan shape (#43, #45)

One always-on layer (organic social, lifecycle messaging, local SEO, referral) with four campaign pulses: C1 Switcher launch (Sep–Oct 2026), C2 Giveaway cycle 1 with built nurture (Oct–Dec, 50%-off close mid-Nov), C3 Giveaway cycle 2 (Jan–Feb 2027), C4 Benchy outdoor launch (Mar–May 2027), then a deliberate winter consolidation (Jun–Aug). Milestones: 220 → ~270 actives at three months → ~330 at six → ~450 at twelve, tracking to 700+ at 24 months and 1,000 at target. The whole calendar is sized to Raef's ~70% allocation, with Jewell carrying builds, paid and production overflow, and recovery months built in by design.

What CP2 sign-off unlocks

Unlocked Detail
Deploy execution of briefs #52–60 Per the #61 critical path: collateral price fix first (this week); CRM (#58) + events capture (#60) wave 1; website, shoot, SEO, EDM seq 1 wave 2; paid launch + PR wave 3; paid scale wave 4
The two hard gates Nothing customer-facing before the price reconciliation; no paid scale before CRM + EDM are live
Budget envelopes Paid media, production, incentives and prize budgets set against P&L data at this checkpoint
The roadmap #64 Approved Roadmap moves from "Pending CP2 sign-off" to live status

# Open item Owner Blocks
1 Membership pricing reconciliation ($250/yr poster vs $300 + $120/yr renewal poster; paddle $18 vs $20) Founders All funnel copy, Keg Crew pitch, renewal flows, LTV maths
2 Full census data + business docs (carried from the discovery session; headline figures only received) Clent → Jules Target locking, the 70%/1–2-kegs claim in customer-facing copy, P&L-derived budgets
3 GoTab/Fishbowl integration date in writing ("pending" is not a date); GoTab × Uber Direct and lease-to-buy timing likewise Justin (with vendors) Lifecycle automation go-live (target Feb 2027), delivery feature, Pour Now Pay Later
4 Competitor verification — "Aardvark & Arrow" spelling and profiles Jewell Any comparative collateral; Google brand-defence check
5 Founder confirmations — capped taproom remit (P3), archetype endorsement, two giveaway closes ~3.5 months apart + prize budget, Benchy pricing/margin (gates Campaign 4), franchise story made public (gates PR story 4), Chris's sign-off on the Burleigh Barrels framing Justin / Chris P3 operating rule, C3 spacing, C4 launch, PR tiers

Why this exists before any results do

Case studies die from evidence not captured at the time. Two of this engagement's stories will be worth telling — but only if the baselines are frozen now, before Deploy moves them, and the capture mechanics run from the first campaign day. This pack names the stories, the numbers, the mechanics and the skeleton. Every results field below is deliberately empty and stays empty until Deploy actuals land.

The two candidate stories

Story A — The switcher campaign Story B — The giveaway-nurture cycle
Working title "We fill any system: acquiring refill customers without selling hardware" "From 22 to [n]: what a 90-day nurture does to a proven giveaway"
The setup 50% of refill customers bought hardware elsewhere and switched unprompted; nobody owned the installed base 2×/yr giveaways pull ~1,000 entries; the 50%-off close converts 22–25 systems; the other ~975 entrants got silence
The intervention A named lane: landing page, Meta behavioural targeting, first-fill offer, counter conversion (C1, Sep–Oct 2026) A designed 90-day sequence matched to the 3-month buying cycle: objection ladder → close window (C2 Oct–Dec 2026, C3 Jan–Feb 2027)
Hypothesis proven (if it works) H1 (switchers beat new-owner CAC) + H2 (kudos opens, cost closes) H3 (structured nurture lifts conversion past 22–25; target 35+)
The headline number, when real Cost per new active vs the hardware-giveaway route; +[n] actives with $0 hardware margin sacrificed Systems per cycle vs the 22–25 baseline, across two cycles (repeatability is the story)

Baselines to freeze on day one (before anything moves)

Baseline Value at 19 Aug 2026 Source to file
Active keg customers (75-day rule) 220 GoTab export, dated
Giveaway conversion 22–25 systems per ~1,000-entry cycle, 50%-off close Last-cycle records + entry list
Churn timing + method Active <75 days / at-risk 75–90 / churned 90+; winback = one hand-sent monthly SMS, $20-off/2-week window Screenshot of the manual SMS; lifecycle definitions doc
Switcher share 50% of refill customers bought hardware elsewhere (client figure — verify via switcher flag once captured) CRM field, first full count
Refill economics ~$200/month per active, ~2 kegs; member 20L $120 vs $150 Photo-verified price board (already filed)
Taproom conversion 20–30% of visitors buy a system (client estimate — instrument in Q2 and re-baseline) GoTab + provenance field
Buying cycle ~60% up to 3 months, ~10% within a month Discovery record
At-risk save / winback rates Unknown — no measurement exists. First quarter of the interim manual layer IS the baseline Weekly export sheet

Capture mechanics (run from day one, owned by the monthly report rhythm)

Mechanic What When
GoTab exports Weekly actives/at-risk/churned counts; per-campaign transaction pulls; the dated day-one baseline export Weekly, filed to 03 Deploy/01 Inputs/evidence/
Screenshots Old conflicting posters (before), reconciled collateral (after); manual SMS thread; leaderboard; ads manager results panels; nurture flow builds; entry-count dashboards At each before/after moment — the "before" shot is the one people forget
Fishbowl/ESP reports Sequence sends, opens, clicks, and the truth metric (transactions in window) per #47 Per cycle
Customer quotes with signed releases Switchers ("why'd you cross the road?"), giveaway buyers, winback returns; capture rides the #54 shoot releases and the content-permission tick on entry forms — no quote is usable without one Ongoing; log quote + release together
Photo/video Winner installs, backyard sessions, Benchy rigs — the #54 library doubles as case-study art Per shoot
Cost records Paid spend per campaign, prize/discount cost per cycle, Jewell + Raef hours — CAC claims need the denominator Monthly

Write-up skeleton (fill only with Deploy actuals)

  1. Context — the at-cost hardware model, margin in refills, 220 actives vs 1,000 target, loss-making P&L, one 70% marketer.
  2. Problem — [Story A: acquisition cost / Story B: the nurture gap]. Stated in the client's own words from discovery.
  3. What we did — the intervention, its cost, its build time, who did what (R/J/C split).
  4. Results — [EMPTY: actives added, conversion vs baseline, cost per active, save/winback rates, revenue run-rate delta]. Every number sourced to a filed export.
  5. Proof texture — quotes (released), before/after screenshots, the repeatability point (cycle 2 vs cycle 1).
  6. What it means — the replicable playbook, honestly caveated (what was luck, what was system).

Where it gets used
